ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-06-sejm-52-1.u36	I'm reopening the session. We continue to consider item 1 of the agenda. Secretary of State at the Ministry of Interior and Administration Maciej Wąsik: Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish government, it is my honor to present... I'm sorry. Although I've asked for silence, these conversations still interfere with the seriousness of the situation. For the second time, I'm asking for it. Secretary of State at the Ministry of Interior and Administration Maciej Wąsik: I have the honour to present to the High House a proposal for a law on special solutions to combat the promotion of aggression against Ukraine and to protect national security. This bill is Polish sanctions against Russia. These are sanctions beyond what the European Union has imposed. It is a proposal of the Polish government to effectively counter the actions of people and economic entities originating in the Russian Federation and Belarus, who directly or even indirectly support Russian military activities in Ukraine, also support the Lukashenka regime, which participates in these activities and the smothering democratic opposition in Belarus. These sanctions, I will repeat this once again, this is something beyond the EU's proposals, it is more than the EU has proposed. Sanctions will consist of four essential elements. The first thing is to freeze all the assets of economic operators. The second element is to limit the exercise of rights from shares in economic entities, any ownership rights. Another element is the exclusion of economic operators from public procurement procedures. One more element is the ban on entry into the territory of the Republic of Poland against persons who will be on the list of sanctions. The list of sanctions will be kept by the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration. The inclusion on this list will take place on the basis of a request from the relevant authority - in this case uniformed, police and special services, as well as institutions, primary bodies, which the Act lists. What are the reasons for the subject being on the list of sanctions? Decisions on listing shall be issued to persons and entities with financial resources, funds and economic resources within the meaning of two EU Regulations, 765 and 269, directly or indirectly supporting the aggression of the Russian Federation in Ukraine and serious human rights violations or repression of civil society in the Russian Federation and Belarus. This decision may also be taken against persons and entities who pose a threat to national security of the Republic of Poland. This list will be kept, as I said, by the Minister of the Interior, in mass communications and on the Internet. For non-compliance with sanctions, i.e. for non-compliance with those sanctions, both administrative and criminal penalties will be threatened. Administrative penalties will be imposed by the National Tax Administration and the Public Procurement Office. These penalties amount to even PLN 20 million in case of non-compliance with the relevant provisions of this law. The Act also prohibits imports of Russian coal into Poland and imposes penalties on entities that do not comply with this provision. [[Hole like a sieve.]] The Act also provides for criminal rules which say that a person who knowingly fails to comply with sanctions may be liable for criminal liability in the form of imprisonment of no less than 3 years. Ladies and gentlemen! We are investigating this law in a situation where Ukraine is under war, when mass graves are found near Kiev, when we are aware that Putin's soldiers commit crimes in the Ukrainian people. We are obliged to take all steps available to the Polish government (The Bell) and Poland to counter this war. This war is financed by economic operators, this war is financed by people who pay Putin their taxes. This law is an important voice of Poland in the European debate on what sanctions should look like. This is an important element that will make the financing of this war much smaller. This is an important element in our quest to isolate Russia economically, because only the economic isolation of Russia can produce the right results. Only that isolation must be introduced at EU level. Ladies and gentlemen! High Seym! Please accept this bill by the Sejm. We are open to discussion, we are open to proposals, we are open to the debate that we are starting today. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] Thank you very much, Minister. I would like to inform you that the Sejm has agreed that in the discussion on this item it will hear five minutes' statements on behalf of the clubs and three minutes' statements on behalf of the circles. ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-06-sejm-52-1.u107	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The problem we've been dealing with for a few weeks is - I suspect - close to each of us, because the child's welfare is the one we always bet the highest, or at least we should bet the highest. I highly appreciate any initiative that leads to the improvement of the child's fate and life situation in this complicated world. The child often suffers completely senseless consequences of disputes that occur between adults, and that should not be the case. Therefore, it is our duty, the responsibility of adults, to make it the duty of legislators to limit these negatives to feel the youngest situations. Therefore, the 1980 Hague Convention introduced certain rules of procedure when a child has to be taken from one of the parents by another parent or from someone who takes care of the child because it so happened by someone who has the right to care. All these elements need to be very carefully considered and resolved. However, always anyone who deals with these matters claims - and so it should be - that the child's well-being is the most important, and the changes that have been proposed by the government are also intended to move in this direction. At Community level, where these issues are regulated by the Brussels IIa Regulation, Member States are invited to reduce the duration of proceedings relating to the issue of a child to the minimum necessary. Even three times after 6 weeks are set in order to fit the proceedings before I and II and to comply with the order. It also draws attention to focusing these cases in several designated courts, where the most experienced judges work and where is the point where cases from different countries can find a common address. Therefore, I very much welcome the appointment of several district courts in Poland to deal with these cases in the first instance and the appointment of one court as the second court in the second instance, here we are talking about the Appeal Court in Warsaw. This is a very good proposal, going in the right direction. On the other hand, the other two proposals pose a problem for me, as they extend the course of action. Their purpose is to introduce a suspension mechanism under the law of enforcement of the final decision of the Court of First Instance to issue a child in the event that the competent authority so requests to file a cassation complaint or in the event of an emergency complaint. While, in the first point of the cassation action, where there is a time limit of 2 weeks after the second instance court's order has been enforced, it is a time limit not too far away, and it is still acceptable for two months to file a possible cassation action, the introduction of a similar mechanism in the event of an emergency complaint seems completely meaningless and in my deep conviction is detrimental to the child. Therefore, I ask that we remove this element from this amendment, and to this extent I make an amendment on behalf of the Civil Coalition Parliamentary Club. As for the other things, we will, of course, be very sympathetic to them during the vote. Thank you, Marshal. Here you go, amendment. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-06-sejm-52-1.u129	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Polish Coalition Club our position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the law - Code of Civil Procedure, print no. 2027 and 2098. This proposal concerns a revision of the provisions on cases recognised under the Hague Convention by introducing a mandatory suspension from office of enforcement of the final order of the ordering court within a specified period of time to ensure the return of the child to the State of habitual residence in the event of a cassation action. The filing by one of the three competent authorities of a cassation complaint shall suspend the final order of the court ordering under the Hague Convention that minors return to the State of their permanent residence. These authorised bodies are the Attorney General, the Ombudsman and the Ombudsman. Such additional protection for the child and the difficulty for the applicant to return the parent provides for the project in question. It therefore alleviates the rigour of family matters related to the abduction of a child - or even to such a charge - abroad. As stated in the explanatory memorandum, it is about safeguarding the child's well-being, ensuring that he is safe and respectful of his dignity and immunity during this painful and usually emotional process. In particular, it is important that a final judgment ordering the return of the child to the State of permanent residence should not be enforced before or at the time of the Cassation action is brought before the Supreme Court. It had, among other things. the place in one of the last cases in which the Attorney General filed a cassation complaint on 9 August 2021, and already on 18 August 2021, the child was forced to take away his mother and returned with his father to his permanent residence in Norway. In such a situation, the cassation action brought turns out to be irrelevant. The draft provides that in cases for the removal of a child at the request of one of the three offices notified to the Court of First Instance, the enforcement of the order will be suspended, except that they must apply for it before the expiry of the time limit for the return of the child no later than 2 weeks. In addition, the lodging of a cassation action by the competent authority shall likewise suspend the order of the court ordering the return of the child to the State of permanent residence within a specified time limit pending the completion of the cassation procedure. The project makes it difficult to issue Polish children abroad in the event of a dispute between parents about taking care of their children. This will allow the judicial authorities to re-examine the case in particularly controversial cases. The purpose of the proposed regulation is to eliminate the situation in which the enforcement of a final judgment before and at the time of the Cassation action is dealt with by the Supreme Court in a state in which the cassation action becomes futile, destroying the effects of the appeal brought in the interest and for the benefit of the child. High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Coalition Club, I request further work.
ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-06-sejm-52-1.u130	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Left Club on the government's bill amending - Code of Civil Procedure in print 2027 together with the report of the Committee on Printing No 2098. The Government's draft law provides for amendments to the provisions on matters recognised under the Hague Convention, in particular those relating to the abduction of a child abroad. If the competent authority lodges a cassation complaint, the new rules will allow the enforcement of the final order of the court, which requires the child to return to the State of habitual residence, to be suspended from office. There are now situations where, both before the cassation action is brought and at the time of its recognition by the Supreme Court, a final decision ordering the return of the child to the State of permanent residence in the course of the proceedings is enforced. This was the case, among others. the place in one of the cases in which, on 9 August 2021, the Attorney General filed a cassation complaint and already on 18 August 2021, the child was forced to take away his mother and returned with his father to his permanent residence in Norway. The cassation action brought in such cases shall be without object to the enforcement of a judgment given under the Hague Convention. These are unique and individual matters, but every such thing is a man's life, the future and the safety of a child. In order to avoid such events in the future, it is necessary to change the rules, because it is not the good of the Member State, not even the good of the parent, but to safeguard the child's well-being here - to ensure that he feels safe and respectful of his dignity and integrity. The most important solutions envisaged by this project. A compulsory institution shall be introduced to suspend from office the enforcement of a final order of the court which requires the return of the child to the State of permanent residence within a specified time limit in the event of a cassation action being brought by the competent authority. In cases of child withdrawal pursuant to the Hague Convention, at the request of the Prosecutor General, the Ombudsman or the Ombudsman, notified to the Court of First Instance, the enforcement of the order shall be suspended. The deadline for submitting a request shall not exceed 2 weeks from the date of the decision's validity. If the court sets a shorter time limit for the return of the child, the eligible entities will have to submit the request more quickly. In order to lodge a cassation complaint resulting in the suspension of enforcement of the order of the Prosecutor General, the Ombudsman or the Ombudsman will have two months from the date of the decision's enforcement. The lodging of a cassation complaint within that period shall extend by law the suspension of enforcement of the order until the end of the cassation procedure. If you don't file a complaint, it's gonna stop. The application of an emergency complaint in the case of child withdrawal pursuant to the Hague Convention will, by law, suspend the enforcement of the order until the action is terminated. Are these solutions needed? This submission responds to the legal problems in the enforcement of final decisions revealed in the practice of investigating cases before the courts under the Hague Convention, but, above all, acts for and for the benefit of the child, increases his sense of security and stability in these exceptional situations, which is why the Left Club supports this solution.

ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-06-sejm-52-1.u198	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Law and Justice Club on the draft law amending the Law - Law on Higher Education and Science and the Law - Provisions introducing the Law - Law on Higher Education and Science, print Nos 2109 and 2141. The aim of the proposed law is to address the most urgent problems arising from the application of certain provisions of the Law - Law on Higher Education and Science, as well as the mitigation of the effects of the first evaluation of the quality of scientific activities, which has already been carried out since 1 January this year. In the case of the Law - Law on Higher Education and Science, the proposed rules cover three categories of key issues. First of all, the consequences of the doctorate's refusal to transfer to another doctoral school in the event of the cessation of doctoral education in a given scientific or artistic discipline. Actually, it's Art. 206 of the Act does not specify the consequences of the doctorate's refusal to continue training at another doctoral school. The proposal therefore proposes the introduction of expressis verbis as a condition for obtaining the doctorate's permission to transfer to another doctoral school and the consequences of not expressing such consent in the absence of a doctoral doctoral degree in another doctoral school in a given discipline, i.e. the obligation for a doctoral practitioner to cover a person who has lost his or her ability to complete his or her education, the costs of the doctoral course in an extraternist manner. The second group of issues concerns the way in which the procedures for granting a doctoral degree are continued in the event of a loss by the habilitating entity of entitlement to such a degree. This is a kind of supplement to a certain legal gap that still exists today. This will depend on the moment of the loss of entitlement. If it occurs before the establishment of the Habitat Committee, then the proceedings will be referred to the body appointed by the Scientific Excellence Board. In the event of a loss of entitlement after the appointment of the Habilitation Committee, the committee will continue its work and the resolution taken by it, together with the remaining documentation of the procedure for granting the degree of doctor of habilitation, shall be forwarded to the body designated by the Scientific Excellence Board. The third issue is the possibility of imposing an administrative penalty on an institution or other entity conducting a doctoral school in the event of a breach of the obligation under Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with the examination procedure referred to in Article 20 (1) of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council. 1 and 2. The last amendment to this law concerns the addition of an article. Article 431 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 point (2) (a) and lips. 2 (1) references to Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with the examination procedure referred to in Article 20 (1) of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council. 1 and 2 and thus the possibility of imposing an administrative penalty on an institution or other entity conducting a doctoral school in the event of a breach of the obligation under Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with the examination procedure referred to in Article 20 (1) of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council. 1 and 2. It will provide the Minister responsible for Higher Education and Science with an additional instrument for monitoring the implementation of PhD students' rights by providing better safeguards. The second major issue that we regulate in this project is the changes proposed in the introductory provisions. They serve to prevent the restrictive effects of the first evaluation, which began in January this year. under the rules laid down in the Act. The proposals also respond to the request of the National Representation of PhD Students for extension by another year - i.e. until the end of this year. - the deadline for the final termination of doctoral courses initiated under the Law on Scientific Degrees and Science Titles and on Degrees and Titles in Arts. At the same time, the provision concerning the closure or termination of promotion proceedings initiated under the provisions in force so far was clarified. The Act introduces a completely new model of evaluation of scientific operators. At the same time, the scope of the activities carried out by the entity will depend on the results of the evaluation, in particular the power to confer degrees or degrees in the field of art and the possibility to train PhD students in doctoral schools in particular disciplines. In view of the above, the proposed law aims to mitigate the effects of the first evaluation. [[♪ The bell ♪]] In view of the arguments put forward, the Law and Justice Club supports these good solutions contained in the draft law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-06-sejm-52-1.u240	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the government's draft law amending the National Court Register Act, print no. 2130. The main objective of this project is to strengthen the information function of the National Court Register, which will allow the safety of legal and economic trade to be enhanced by allowing interested parties to obtain immediately information on the impact on registration files of applications or other documents submitted by unauthorised persons and on the entry in the register. In particular, it concerns requests for changes to alerts on the manner in which the entity is represented and on persons representing the entity whose documents can be forged. The purpose of the Act is therefore to prevent theft of companies. On the basis of the proposed provisions, technical solutions will be put in place to allow interested parties to obtain information on the initiation or termination of a registered case before the application for registration or the enforcement of a decision is made. That way, if you use forged documents... [[I can't hear you. Louder.]] ...the entitled persons will be able to take part in the ongoing proceedings, challenge the judgment of the alert, and also notify the law enforcement authorities of the attempted theft of the company. You can hear it now, can't you? Thank you for your attention. How will the bill implement this request? It is about adding art to the Act. 10a, which provides that information on the registration of a case including the KRS number, the case signature and the date on which the case is registered and the entry in the register will be automatically transmitted via the electronic system to anyone who has identified the KRS number of the entity to whom the information is intended. In view of the purpose of this law, i.e., as I said, above all the prevention of theft of companies, the Law and Justice support these changes. We will, of course, vote in favour of this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-06-sejm-52-1.u241	Thank you very much. Ms Katarzyna Osos will present the position of the Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-06-sejm-52-1.u242	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Coalition Club, I will present my position on the draft law amending the Act on the National Court Register. The aim of this project is to reduce illegal acquisitions of companies, i.e. so-called. theft of companies, which most often occur by changing the entry in the National Court Register on the basis of forged documents. The proposed changes are intended to seal the system by introducing a newsletter service sent by email. This service will enable entrepreneurs to access information about registered changes made in the company. Events such as registration of a particular entity or entering an entry in the National Court Register will be reported. The person who receives this information will be able to consult the details of the case by looking at the register files or up-to-date or complete information about the entity entered in the KRS. Therefore, the newsletter will allow the entrepreneur to react quickly and - which is the purpose of the legislation - to thwart fraud, of course with the help of law enforcement. The solutions presented certainly provide an additional tool to prevent economic crime. Of course, we are aware that this solution will not be reliable, but it is important that it reduces the so-called phenomenon. Theft of companies. The Civic Coalition will therefore be in favour of the proposed amendments. Thank you. [[Applause]]

Because as we know, businessmen and judges and referenda, and judicial staff have struggled with various problems in the operation of this system, e.g. could not be deleted from the company register. Well, now it's time for another shift. This time, it is intended to add an article to the National Court Register Act. 10a providing for the possibility of automatic transmission through the IT system of information on registration of a case in registration proceedings, the registration of an entity entered in the register of entrepreneurs, associations, other social and professional organisations, foundations and independent public health establishments. This amendment is intended to prevent theft of companies, which is a tricky move to change the entry in the boxes of the National Court Register concerning the representation of the entity. In such cases, the decisions shall be based on false documents. Such cases occur in practice, as we sometimes learn from the media. Persons who commit so-called "clusters'. thefts of the company use ignorance of legitimate members of the organization and shareholders of the companies, it is a matter of submitting a request to the registry court for the change of entries concerning the representation of the company and the change of data in the KRS. This amendment provides for the introduction of a kind of KRS newsletter to prevent fraudsters from taking over companies. The newsletter service is to automatically send information about events such as registration of a specific entity and entering an entry in the register to interested parties via the electronic system operating the proceedings. The person who receives this information will be able to consult the details of the case by reviewing the register files or up-to-date or complete information about the entity. In order to be informed by the newsletter, the user of the platform account will have to indicate the KRS number to which the information is to be transmitted via the IT system supporting the judicial proceedings. In the event of the use of falsified documents, the entitled persons will therefore be able to take part in the ongoing proceedings, to challenge an alert decision and to notify the law enforcement authorities of the attempted so-called. Theft of the company. Finally, although the designers assume that the main purpose of the service is to prevent theft of companies, this solution will also enable a stage of proceedings by a registered court to be established, which will in fact relieve the judicial secretariats. In view of the above, because we believe that the proposed changes are mainly technical in nature, but are also moving in the right direction, they increase legal certainty - and as a Left we are also in favour of computerisation in public life, both in public administration and in common courts - we therefore support this presentation. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-06-sejm-52-1.u266	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! The draft law aims to introduce into the legal order the arrangements resulting from the agreements concluded in 2018 and 2021 between the Minister for Home Affairs and Administration and the social party represented by representatives of trade unions representing the formation of the Ministry of Home Affairs and Administration and between the Minister of Justice and the Independent Self-Governmental Trade Union Officers and Prison Workers. These solutions are intended to enable officers, as well as professional soldiers admitted to service for the first time after 1 January 1999 and before 1 October 2003, who have at least a period of 25 years of service on the day of their release, calculated together with equivalent periods, to be included in the pension service so-called. periods of civil service. Currently, in the case of officers, but also professional soldiers appointed for service after 1 January 1999, the amount of the pension shall not be taken into account prior to the entry into service for premium and non-contributory periods and periods of payment of pension contributions after the discharge of the service. For these periods of civil service, an official may be retired after reaching retirement age. common with the Social Insurance Fund. This means that there are currently two pension systems for officials: the uniform system and the insurance system. The draft law aims to adopt a uniform uniform system by transferring existing pension obligations from the general system to this system. It should be stressed that the draft law establishes the possibility for an officer to choose the rules for calculating the pension. In the absence of a choice, the officer will retire on the basis of the existing rules. The draft law was submitted for opinion to trade unions operating in different formations. The general conclusion of the consultation is that the solutions to the project do not meet the expectations of the officers as they do not correspond to the commitments accepted by the MMA and contained in the signed agreement. Officers' requests are aimed at restoring solutions before 1999, as they were more favourable, among other things. 15 years, not 25 years of service and indicators for the first 3 years of pre-service work - 2.6% , not 1,3% It's like a project. When analysing the draft law and listening to the opinions of the representatives of the uniform services, it should be stressed that the changes introduced in the draft agree with those proposed by the trade unions in terms of the direction of the solutions, but the project does not take into account all expectations. The negotiations lasted for several years and in the essential part did not end with an agreement. In our view, a compromise should be found in this dispute, taking into account, at least partially, the expectations of trade unionists. That's why we're making amendments to the bill. The parliamentary club will vote in favour of the bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-06-sejm-52-1.u330	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to start by apologizing for being late, but there is a significant acceleration in today's proceedings. Ladies and gentlemen, the law on assistance to Ukrainian citizens in connection with the armed conflict in the territory of Ukraine was adopted in order to create a specific legal framework providing the legal basis for legal residence for Ukrainian citizens who, as a result of the war, were forced to leave their country of origin. The solutions that have been adopted have been adopted in a very short time since the beginning of the attack on the territory of the Russian Federation troops, and these have been solutions prepared jointly by the ministries, which coordinate on many issues, such as health care, health care, work resort, other departments, families and social policy, as well as by the local government. So from the beginning these first solutions were prepared together, you can even say: as never. This law was passed on 12 March. The period of validity of this law has brought experience indicating the need to clarify certain of its provisions, as well as to supplement the issues omitted in the adopted draft. The needs were reported by both local authorities and resorts, i.e. Ministry of Family and Social Policy, Ministry of Education and Science, law firm of the Prime Minister, as well as voivodships, representatives of the government in the field. The project we are presenting today includes proposals to clarify and adapt certain provisions of the special provisions to the current situation that arose in the context of the mass influx of foreigners from the territory of Ukraine, and also complements certain issues which have not been covered by the current law. Its main changes are the question of extending the scope of the Act on the distribution of medicinal products intended for humanitarian aid in Ukraine by the Government's Strategic Reserve Agency. It is a matter of temporary admission of Ukrainians to the profession of doctor, to help just those who come, to foreigners who come from Ukraine. It is a question of clarifying the rules for granting the status of a beneficiary of the law. It is a question of enabling the informal authorisation of persons not employed in municipal offices to carry out tasks related to the transmission of the PESEL number; it is intended to speed up the operation of the PESEL number; it is also a joint operation of government and local government administration. It is also a question of posting workers in the wider public sector, so that they can support bodies carrying out activities related to the PESEL number. This is a clarification of many different provisions that have appeared in the original form of the law, which are in force today, and as a result of the implementation of this law, need to be clarified. This enables the use of hospital medicinal products used in health programmes, in health policy programmes, for compulsory treatment in favour of home pharmacotherapy patients. This is a clarification of the regulations concerning the possession by persons to whom the PESEL number has been assigned by the provisions of the amended Act, i.e. those persons... Any issues related to health care and assistance before obtaining the PESEL number. These are many, many different solutions that we have adopted after consultation with public administration institutions, which help the Ukrainians every day. It is also the creation of a National Crisis Guarantee Fund in Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego, under which guarantees and guarantees of repayment of loans or other commitments entered into by entrepreneurs may be granted. This solution was adopted on the basis of legislation on the current liquidity guarantee fund in BGK, which was created due to the situation caused by the COVID pandemic. A similar solution, as it was in COVID-z. Ladies and gentlemen! We are open to discussion, to further discussion also about this law, the subsequent (The Bell) amendments to this law. And that's what they found. On 12 March the Polish Government prepared a bill, and the Sejm passed a law on assistance to Ukrainian citizens in connection with armed conflict in the territory of the country. This law has been passed in order to create a specific regulation providing an ad hoc legal basis for the legal residence of Ukrainian citizens in the territory of our country. And, as usual, in such difficult times, the application of such regulations is subject to circumstances which make the provisions of the law necessary to be amended. Here I thank the government, Minister Chefner, for preparing this amendment, because indeed, representatives of local governments, NGOs have asked us to do a number of times, suggesting that there should be some changes that will make it easier for individual entities to take care of people who need help from the Polish State. As the Minister said, this project affects many, many areas of activity of the Polish government, starting from the educational area. The issue of enabling children coming from Ukraine to fulfil their schooling obligations is clarified here, as well as certain safeguards are being implemented by means of this law. Many regulations in this law are devoted to the area related to health protection. On the basis of the existing regulations, Ukrainian citizens who were registered with medical education were able to provide benefits in the Polish health system, provided that they had the ability to communicate in the Polish language. This draft law clarifies that Ukrainian citizens who have medical qualifications will be able to provide benefits, in particular to Ukrainian citizens, even if they do not speak Polish fluently. The regulations that are included in this draft law allow, among others. clarify the issue of the PESEL number. This is a question of excluding Polish citizens from the group of people who were to be subject to the regulation of the specialization, because the current regulations require that Polish citizens also broadcast the status of UKR in the register of PESEL and collect their biometric data when the Polish citizen is e.g. spouse of a Ukrainian citizen. The scope of the Act on the Distribution of Medicinal Products by the Government's Strategic Reserve Agency for Humanitarian Aid transferred to Ukraine is also extended. As regards support for the use of medicines, it is also possible to use hospital pharmacies for medicinal products used in health programmes, health policy programmes or compulsory treatment for pharmacotherapy patients at home. There are also a number of regulations that clarify the way in which the funding is made available to finance the costs incurred by the service to ensure the safety of refugees from the fund. The provisions of the Act also allow for the authorisation of persons not employed in municipal offices or cities to carry out activities related to the broadcasting of PESEL numbers to Ukrainian citizens. The Act also gives the voivodeship the opportunity to organize emergency medical assistance in places where Ukrainian citizens residing in Poland in connection with war activities gather. This law also contains provisions that clarify the provisions of the Sign Language Act and other means of communication in terms of funding from the State Fund for the Rehabilitation of Persons with Disabilities. High Izbo! When I hear all of my previous speakers, I thought, and I remembered a quote. Kennedy once said that humanity must put an end to war, or war will put an end to humanity. When we go deeper into all aspects of the cruelty of this armed conflict in Ukraine, these words are indeed of particular importance. Today we are working on a law aimed at introducing a package of measures that extends sanctions directly at national level. As government officials say, banking institutions have so far frozen assets and assets resulting directly from EU sanctions of about PLN 160 million, following sanctions by the European Union. If we would like to speak in substance about the Act, it should be recalled that the freezing of funds will concern cash, cheques, transfers of funds, deposits made in financial institutions or other entities. It will also cover securities, bonds, shares and shares. On the other hand, the freezing of economic resources will apply to assets of any kind, material and intangible, movable and immovable, which are funds but can be used to obtain funds, goods or services. The entities that will be included in the list of sanctions will not be able to carry out public contracts, but will also not be able to carry out competitions conducted under the Act of 11 September 2019 - Public Procurement Law. An entry on the list of sanctions will be made by the Minister of the Interior either of his own motion or at the request of the special services and certain institutions, including, for example, at the request of the CBA heads, the ABW, the intelligence agency, the Military Counterintelligence Service and others, the national prosecutor, the Financial Supervision Commission and the President of the National Bank of Poland. The Act also provides that failure to decide to freeze will involve penalties - financial penalties and criminal penalties. The financial penalty will be up to PLN 20 million, while this more important penalty will concern the possibility of imprisonment up to 3 years. It should be mentioned that the freezing of funds and economic resources will apply to persons and entities listed other than persons and entities listed in the European Union Regulations 765/2006 and 269/2014. Another element of this law, which, I think, is at the heart of the whole legislative procedure, is the embargo imposed on imports of coal, which will concern imports from the territory of the Russian Federation, Belarus, as well as through transit from the countries of the European Union. It's very important. All the more so, as the Polish People's Party, we stress here that at this moment all without exception, regardless of the political ointment, must consider this fact and the quick possibility of solving the problems related to energy deficits. Therefore, we call for our law, which was submitted to the Marshal's staff, to be processed, to be thwarted by the process of using RES. I think that this is where we should consider, High Izbo, whether we need to move towards an Energy Union within the European Union. Maybe it's a solution, a quick solution to this energy crisis that we're drawing on the horizon. My group, my club, of course. will support this law, but we will certainly watch very closely the implementation of the provisions of this law and we hope that the conflict in Ukraine will resolve quickly and people will be able to live, develop and work safely. ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-06-sejm-52-1.u483	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I want to talk to you today about the bloody pacification in Jawornik in the Thinking County on the 78th anniversary of its implementation. Organized on the basis of the pre-war administrative division in the circuits, the district of Thinkenicki received the code name ¿Muraważ. The first classic guerrilla troops appeared on thinking ground in 1943. One of the heroes of those terrible days was Józef Fijałkowski. He became famous as a charismatic and courageous soldier. His unit was famous, among others. an action in Jawornik, during which he set an ambush for the German column heading towards Sułkowice. The order the guerrillas carried out by attacking the convoy was to prevent the pacification of Sulkowice. Two German MPs and three high-ranking German officers were killed in the ambush. In retaliation, the German invaders, as was the case in other towns of the county of thought, decided to take revenge on the helpless local population, organizing a bloody pacification of Jawornik. In a barbaric way they murdered 12 people, including three children: Kazimiera Tomal, Maria Tomal, Rozalia Tomal, Anna Tomal, Eugeniusz Puttelnik, Maria Puttelnik, Stanisława Kasperczyk, Andrzej Węgrzyn, 72 years old, Anna Węgrzyn, Andrzej Węgrzyn, 15 years old, Jana Węgrzyn and one guerrilla with unknown personalities. Thirteen buildings belonging to Hungarians, Tomals, Hermits and Kasperks were burned to the ground. Death was avoided by Jan Węgrzyn and Jan Tomal, who survived for decades after the war, testifying to this tragedy. Jan Węgrzyn escaped from the manhunt and even though he was wounded, he survived. The story is even more dramatic. Jan Tomal, who miraculously avoided death because the bullet hit his hat instead of the head, and before that, the bullets from the machine gun didn't reach him. Without betraying himself, he was lying there pretending to be killed. With the bodies of the victims, he was taken to the burial site, and he, using the convenient moment, escaped. The experience of terror on the part of the occupier and the cruelty that the occupier used against the innocent and defenseless population of villages and cities during the war proves how massive the scale of repression was, how great crimes were committed on Poles. The pacification in Jawornik was included in a precisely planned project to destroy the Polish people. Although these are painful experiences, we must not forget them because we need to protect ourselves and save our loved ones from the repetition of the events that war and human hatred have given us. The memory of the victims of this crime, whose innocent blood was spilled by German invaders, is alive in Jawornik and its surroundings until today. In 2012, 68 years after the tragedy that affected the village, the obelisk commemorating the victims of the pacification of 4 April 1944 was unveiled and dedicated. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The monument was created thanks to the initiative of the Soviet Council in Jawornik, parish and financial assistance of the municipality of Myślenice. Hello their memory! [[Applause]]
